NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, April 9, 2019
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, April 9, 2019, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: March 26, 2019  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 March 2019  
 
c. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, March 2019  
 
d. Personnel Actions  
 
e. Reissuance of Stale-Dated Checks  
 
f. Approval of 5 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $2,711.86  
 
g. Appointment of Audrey L. Bevers and Jeffrey S. Bevers as Republican Precinct
Committeemen for their respective precinct
 
 
h. Special Event Liquor License to Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce for their annual 4th
of July activities to be held at the Tall Timbers Park in Heber-Overgaard
 
 
i. Special Event Liquor License to Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce for their annual

<!-- page 2 -->
i. Special Event Liquor License to Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce for their annual
Oktoberfest Event to be held on September 28 and 29 at the Tall Timbers Park in
Heber-Overgaard
 
 
j. Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver  
 
k. Fiscal Year 2019 Fund Transfers  
 
3. EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION:  
 
a. Presentation on the activities of the CARE Committee.  
Presented By: Ron Smith
 
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Recognition of Jason Owens for receiving the Arizona County Engineer's Association
Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support 
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
b. Presentation of APS Wildfire Mitigation.  
Presented By: Neil Traver, Division Manager
 
5. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Budget Presentations 
a. Public Works/Planning & Zoning - John Osgood
b. Navajo County Public Health Services District, Board of Director's Session - Jeff Lee
c. Emergency Management - Catrina Jenkins
 
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
6. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. AZ Prescription Drug Overdose Prevention Program grant Amendment 1  
 
2. Contract Number CTF042784 for the Emergency Preparedness Program  
 
3. Contract Number CTR042871 for the Tuberculosis Control Program  
 
4. Contract Number CTR042929 for the HIV Prevention Program  
 
5. Contract Number CTR043064 for the Smoke Free Arizona Program  
 
6. Contract Number CTR043084 for the STD Control Program

<!-- page 3 -->
b. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Presentation on the Open & Engaged Community Conversation May 20, 2019.  
Presented By: Allison Hephner
 
2. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring April 2019 “Child Abuse Prevention Month” in
Navajo County.
 
Presented By: Jeff Lee
 
7. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 14 to Memorandum of Agreement between
the Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation- Navajo Region and Navajo County
for the funding of purchasing roadway signs for BIA route 15 within the Dilkon Community. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
b. Consideration of award of Contract B19-01-009 Woodruff Concrete Bridge Repairs to Protech
Coatings, Inc. dba Infrastructure Maintenance Services.
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
c. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution:
Amendment Number 1, Contract for Professional Services, Advocate for the Little Colorado at
Winslow Levee
 
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
9. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: April 3, 2019 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 12:30 pm By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.g.        
Meeting Date: 04/09/2019  
Title: Elections REP PC Appointments
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Audrey L. Bevers and Jeffrey S. Bevers as Republican Precinct
Committeemen for their respective precinct
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2020. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility;
vacancy; duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an
election to fill precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of
precinct committeeman positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election
for those positions not sooner than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the
person who filed the nominating petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a
nominating petition to fill a position, the position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as
otherwise provided by law. A precinct committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this
subsection after filing a nominating petition shall be deemed an elected precinct
committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is two years and begins
on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman was a
candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.

<!-- page 426 -->
THE “CARE” REPORT 2018 
 
Awesome Navajo County Employees 
 
The following CARE Committee projects have 
touched so many lives in 2018 … and we couldn’t 
have done it without your help!  Join the more 
than 125 employees who help each month by 
donating a dollar or more each payday. It’s easy 
– just fill out the form on the back and turn it in 
to Human Resources  today! If you are already 
donating, THANK YOU!  ☺ 
 
 
Employee Hardship Assistance 
Program 
 
31 employee families were assisted in their time 
of need during 201 8 through the Employee 
Hardship Assistance Program. These employees 
who experienced a serious illness, d eath in the 
family, or other hardship,  were sent a card and 
had $50.00 added to their paycheck. “It helps 
when you’re sick in the hospital or have a death 
in the family to know that someone cares.” We 
can only help if were made aware of situation.  
 
Backpack Drive 
 
33 children from 12 families of Navajo County 
employees went back to school with a backpack 
filled with supplies for the new school year.  
 
Random Acts of Kindness 
 
Each month , six employee names are drawn 
from a list of those celebrating birthdays that 
month. Those employees receive a tumbler/cup 
filled with candy. That  was 72 employees who 
received a pleasant “surprise” in 2018. 
 
Employee Recognition Program 
 
34 employees were rec ognized in 201 8 as 
“Employees of the Month.” Each received a $50 
gift card and a certificate . A leaf engraved with 
their name was also added to the “Tree of 
Excellence” in the Board of Supervisors’ room.  
 
Employee of the Year Recognition 
Program 
 
All employees who are nominated for Employee 
of the Month and still employed by Navajo 
County are entered in an end of the year prize 
drawing for one (1) $500 gift card and two (2) 
$250.00 gift cards. That’s $ 1,000.00 going to 
employees who have excelled in their job and 
have been nominated by fellow employees for a 
job well done. Do you know someone in your 
department who follows the 5 Strategic Values 
of Teamwork, Accountability, Integrity, 
Excellence, & Innovation? Then go to the internal 
web and nominate them. *Remember, the more 
information, the better.   
 
Angel Tree 
 
50 children from 18 families of Navajo County 
employees received gifts for Christmas. This was 
more than twice as many as 2017.  
Members 
Want to make a difference in the lives of your co-
workers (work family)? Come join us! We meet 
the first Wednesday of the month @ 1:30 P.M . 
Many hands make light work for all. 
It only takes a 
DOLLAR to show you 
care!      
“CARE”  
Continually Achieving & 
Reaching Employees of 
Navajo County

<!-- page 427 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 4.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/09/2019  
Title: Recognition of Jason Owens
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Bill Bess
Subject:
Recognition of Jason Owens for receiving the Arizona County Engineer's Association
Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support 
Background: 
The Arizona Association of County Engineers (AACE) Board of Directors, has established the
Robert C. Esterbrooks Award to recognize individuals whose contributions to the public through
the State’s County Public Works Departments who have made a difference in the lives of
friends, family, coworkers, colleagues and the public. 
 
Jason Owens was nominated by Bill Bess and Brandt Clark for the 19th Annual Robert C.
Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support.  Jason is a fabulous employee, a huge asset to
Navajo County and is so deserving of this award and recognition.
 
The Engineering Support Award is open state wide to Public Works; Engineering Technicians,
Surveyors, Survey Technicians, GIS Analysts, CADD Operators, Right of Way Agents,
Inspectors, and Administrative Assistants.  The nomination needed to demonstrate how Jason;
made a significant contribution Navajo County; is an exemplary Navajo County employee; uses
innovative approaches/methods; and enhances the image of Navajo County.  Attached is a copy
of Jason’s Nomination Letter.
 
We submitted Jason’s nomination and shortly thereafter were notified he was winner 19th
Annual Robert C. Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support.  At the AACE Conference
Award Dinner on March 28, 2019, Jason was presented the AACE Award Plaque.  Jason’s wife,
Robin, Brandt Clark, Sandy Phillips and Bill Bess were present at the dinner to see the Award
presentation.

<!-- page 429 -->
BACKGROUND TEXT 
 
The Arizona Association of County Engineers (AACE) Board of Directors, has established the 
Robert C. Esterbrooks Award to recognize individuals whose contributions to the public through 
the State’s County Public Works Departments who have made a difference in the lives of 
friends, family, coworkers, colleagues and the public.   
 
Jason Owens was nominated by Bill Bess and Brandt Clark for the 19th Annual Robert C. 
Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support.  Jason is a fabulous employee, a huge asset to 
Navajo County and is so deserving of this award and recognition.  
 
The Engineering Support Award is open state wide to Public Works; Engineering Technicians, 
Surveyors, Survey Technicians, GIS Analysts, CADD Operators, Right of Way Agents, 
Inspectors, and Administrative Assistants.  The nomination needed to demonstrate how the 
nominee; made a significant contribution Navajo County; is an exemplary Navajo County 
employee; uses innovative approaches/methods; and enhances the image of Navajo County.  
Attached is a copy of Jason’s Nomination Letter. 
 
We submitted Jason’s nomination and shortly there after were notified he was winner 19th 
Annual Robert C. Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support.  At the AACE Conference Award 
Dinner on March 28, 2019, Jason was presented an Award Plaque.  Jason’s wife, Robin, Brandt 
Clark and Bill Bess were present at the dinner to see the Award presentation.

<!-- page 430 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY 
Public Works Department 
 
“Best Customer Service ” 
  
• 928.524.4106 • Fax: 928.524.4122 • P.O. Box 668 • Holbrook, AZ 86025 •  
•bill.bess@navajocountyaz.gov • www.navajocountyaz.gov • 
  
 
January 31, 2019   
 
 
Sheila Hamilton 
AACE Executive Director 
Arizona Association of County Engineers 
PO Box 72720 
Phoenix, AZ 85050 
 
 
RE: Robert C. Esterbrooks Award for Engineering Support  
Mr. Jason Owens Nomination 
 
 
Dear Nomination Committee, 
 
I am writing this letter to nominate Jason Owens for the Robert C. Esterbrooks Award for 
Engineering Support.  He is a fabulous employee, a huge asset to Navajo County and is so 
deserving of this award and recognition.  Let me tell you a little about him. 
 
Significant Contributions to County  
Jason Owens has worked for the Navajo County Public Works Department for Five (5) years.  
Jason is the Engineering Project Manager for the Navajo County Public Works Department.  In 
this capacity, Jason primary duties include; Senior Civil 3D AutoCAD Designer; Senior Survey 
technician with land and construction survey experience; Certified Cartography technician and 
Licensed drone pilot.  
Jason is given a project assignment.  He coordinates with other departments and schedules the 
time needed to complete the project.  Jason completes the field work for the horizontal and 
vertical control, he flies the project with using the drone, downloads the data into Civil 3D, 
completes the design and then provides the plan for my review.    
In my years of experience in the engineering department, Jason’s knowledge of project 
management is unparalleled.  He seems to be a walking encyclopedia of knowledge when it 
comes to surveying and mapping, and he loves to share his knowledge with anyone who will 
listen.  In the past year, we’ve been assigned a number of projects needing precise surveying 
and mapping that affect the citizens of Navajo County.  Before I could even tell him to, Jason 
already begins the preliminary steps needed to address these projects. Throughout the time I 
have worked with Jason, he has consistently met established project deadlines and milestones.  
I don’t know of anyone else more qualified to lend his expertise in the area of surveying,  civil 
engineering design and mapping than Jason. 
Despite the overwhelming amount of work that everyone has to do, Jason consistently finds 
ways to help his co-workers.  His willingness to assist others and his commitment to educating 
Navajo County employees is unequaled.  Staff members from other offices and departments

<!-- page 431 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY 
Public Works Department 
 
“Best Customer Service ” 
  
• 928.524.4106 • Fax: 928.524.4122 • P.O. Box 668 • Holbrook, AZ 86025 •  
•bill.bess@navajocountyaz.gov • www.navajocountyaz.gov • 
  
Plan have all noted that Jason is thorough in his projects, providing great detail about the 
subject matter, and he consistently follows-through to ensure understanding of complex 
matters to other team members who might have an in-depth understanding of the projects he 
completes.  He is quick to respond to requests for additional information by other team 
members and is thorough in ensuring all loose ends are completed.   
 
Exemplary Employee 
I have the pleasure of knowing Mr. Owens for more than Five (5) years now. During this time I 
have had the pleasure of working with Mr. Owens in both a business, and personal setting.  
Throughout the time I have known Mr. Owens, I have found him to be a very thoughtful, 
intelligent, creative person who has always sought opportunities to better himself.   
Mr. Owens is a hard worker and dedicates long hours to the betterment of Navajo County.  It is 
not uncommon for Mr. Owens to work late in order to complete a time sensitive projects.  
When Jason first started working at Navajo County, the former Public Works Director and I 
observed Mr. Owens setting up his survey equipment in the late afternoon, when most 
surveyors would be packing their equipment away.  The then Public Works Director commented 
on his amazement that Jason was still working on the project late into the evening.  We were a 
little surprise at this type of can-do attitude when Jason first started working for Navajo County, 
but after more than five (5) years working with Jason, this work ethic hasn’t diminished at all.  
Although Jason does not have the official designation of “supervisor” in his job title, he has 
proven himself to be the go-to-guy for all others in the public works department. He regularly 
volunteers to mentor new hires, and “show them the ropes.”   
Mr. Owens is a critical thinker and problem solver.  During the time I have known Mr. Owens he 
has always been a problem solver.  Many times, I have seen Mr. Owens in diverse group 
settings, when tasked with various problems, many people are quick to give the perceived right 
answer without much thought.  However, Mr. Owens analyzes the problem and thinks through 
the problem, looking at the problem in different ways than his peers.  He is one to think outside 
the box and come up with answers others in the group did not even consider. This usually 
requires critical independent thinking, and the need to work long hours on a project to ascertain 
the appropriate result.  I have seen Mr. Owens do this on numerous occasions.  
 
Uses Innovative Approaches/Methods 
Navajo County has a thriving Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAV), or drones as they are commonly 
referred to, program that is recognized as the leader in innovation and development throughout 
the State of Arizona. The reason we even have a drone program is because of Jason Owens. The 
first drone Navajo County started flying was one Jason won at a surveying workshop.  Jason 
started flying his own personal drone for Public Works projects and would upload the 
information during his spare time.  After a few projects, the decision makers at Navajo County 
recognized the value of this innovative technology.  Jason worked with these decision makers to 
develop a drone program for all of Navajo County.  What started out as Jason using a drone he 
won at the workshop, has turned into a thriving program that is expanding every day.

<!-- page 432 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY 
Public Works Department 
 
“Best Customer Service ” 
  
• 928.524.4106 • Fax: 928.524.4122 • P.O. Box 668 • Holbrook, AZ 86025 •  
•bill.bess@navajocountyaz.gov • www.navajocountyaz.gov • 
  
Currently, Navajo County Public Works Department has two (2) operating drones, and the 
Navajo County Sheriff’s Office has five (5) operating drones.  These drones are used for a wide 
variety of projects, including aerial photography, site surveying, stockpile volume calculations, 
crash scene evaluations, construction planning and management, bridge inspection, 
communication tower inspection, search and rescue, crime scene investigation, surveillance, 
drug task force reconnaissance, SWAT operations, and the list continues to expand.   
With Jason’s expertise, Navajo County developed policies and procedures to standardize their 
drone program. These policies and procedures were adopted by the Arizona Counties Insurance 
Pool (ACIP) and utilized by any county desiring to develop a drone program that is represented 
by ACIP.  None of this would have happened without Jason Owens diligence and foresight.  
 
Enhances the Image of the County  
Jason works with the local middle school and high school to promote the area of engineering 
and drones. He currently advises the local high school drone club and offers his insight to future 
drone pilots.  
Jason works with the school’s robotics club and trains the students to use AutoCAD to design 
their projects 
With Jason’s already busy schedule with work, drone technology and family, he also finds time 
to be active with the Boy Scouts.  He works with young men to help them earn their merit 
badges as well supervising camping outings. 
He has become a trusted advisor to these individuals and they look to him for guidance and 
leadership.   
 
Why Jason Owens 
Not once since I have been Jason’s supervisor have I heard one negative comment about him.  It 
is with great pleasure that I would like to nominate Jason Owens for the Robert C. Esterbrooks 
Award for Engineering Support. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
William R. Bess, P.E., CFM 
Assistant Public Works Director / County Engineer 
 
 
c: John Osgood, Public Works Direc tor

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.a.1.

Title: AZ Prescription Drug Overdose Prevention Program Amendment 1
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director 
Submitted By: Jennifer Flake, Health Department Finance Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
AZ Prescription Drug Overdose Prevention Program grant Amendment 1
Background:
Abuse and addiction to opioids is a serious and challenging national public health problem.  For
the last 3 years the Health District has utilized this grant to educate providers on the importance of
monitoring opioid prescriptions and entering the information in the statewide database.  The funds
have also been used to provide community education, drug lockers and various educational
materials.
This amendment updates the price sheet and increases the scope of work to include the
development of an opioid fatality review team (with a quarterly report requirement).
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 20
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
All activities are grant funded.  No fiscal impact to the county.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.b.2.        
Meeting Date: 04/09/2019  
Title: Consideration of a Proclamation declaring April 2019 “Child Abuse Prevention
Month” in Navajo County
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director  Submitted By: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Presented By: Jeff Lee
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring April 2019 “Child Abuse Prevention Month” in Navajo
County.
Background:
National Child Abuse Prevention Month recognizes the importance of families and communities
working together to prevent child abuse and neglect and promotes the social and emotional
well-being of children and families. During the month of April, communities are encouraged to
increase awareness and provide education and support to families through resources and strategies
to prevent child abuse and neglect. Each year, the White House and many states, counties, cities
and towns issue proclamations to raise awareness and to encourage communities to take steps to
improve the well-being of children. 
Recommendation: 
Approve Proclamation declaring April 2019 “Child Abuse Prevention Month” in Navajo County
Attachments
Proclamation

<!-- page 472 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
  Child Abuse Prevention Month 
WHEREAS, National Child Abuse Prevention Month will be recognized throughout the United 
States, during the month of April, 2019; and 
WHEREAS, preventing child abuse and neglect is a community problem that depends on 
involvement among people throughout the community; and 
WHEREAS, during periods of economic challenges, families feel more vulnerable, and as a result, 
child abuse and neglect increases drastically; and 
WHEREAS, child abuse and neglect can be reduced by making sure each family has the support 
they need to raise their children in a healthy environment; and 
WHEREAS, child abuse is considered to be one of our nation’s most serious public health 
problems with scientific studies documenting the link between the abuse and neglect of children 
and a wide range of medical, emotional, psychological and behavioral disorders; and  
WHEREAS, all citizens should become involved in strengthening families to raise their children in 
a safe and nurturing environment; and 
WHEREAS, effective child abuse prevention programs succeed because of partnerships created 
among state and local government agencies, schools, faith communities, civic organizations, law 
enforcement agencies, and the business community; and 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County and the 
Navajo County Public Health Services District do hereby proclaim the month of April 2019, as 
“Child Abuse Prevention Month ” in Navajo County, Arizona, I urge all citizens, community 
agencies, faith groups, medical facilities, and businesses to increase their participation in our 
efforts to strengthen families, thereby preventing child abuse and creating healthier 
communities. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on _______, 20__. 
 
         ____________________________ 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/09/2019  
Title: BIA 15 Sign Replacement Agreement
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 14 to Memorandum of Agreement between the
Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation- Navajo Region and Navajo County for
the funding of purchasing roadway signs for BIA route 15 within the Dilkon Community. 
Background:
The Dilkon Community is requesting assistance and guidance on safety improvements at the
intersection of BIA 15 and State Route 87 after recent fatalities have occurred due to drivers not
being aware of upcoming intersection at night. In 2018, Arizona Division of Transportation
replied to the Road Safety Assessment (RSA) request that Navajo County submitted by supplying
a consultant to create a study of the intersection.  By summer 2018, WSP, Inc. finalized their
report on the intersection and one of the recommendations they had was replacing the outdated
signs that were on Indian Route 15. WSP, Inc. developed design plans for placement of the signs
on route 15. Navajo County will purchase the signs that WSP, Inc. recommended for BIA 15 and
Fort Defiance Agency (BIA) has agreed to place them into the ground. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends that the Board of Supervisors approve the attached Task Agreement between
BIA DOT and Navajo County. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 20,000
Fiscal Impact:
District 2 Navajo Nation CIP Funds: $20,000

Task Agreement NO. 14 
Bureau of Indian Affairs- Fort Defiance Agency 
Sign Replacement Route 15 
 
The  Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular 
meeting on April 9, 2019, has approved the expenditure of up to $20,000 in ( ) Special 
Project Road Funds, or (X) Highway User Revenue Funds to be used for funding the 
purchase of Roadway Signs  for road maintenance activity for a road safety assessment 
provided by ADOT on the intersection of BIA Route N15/ State Route 87, which is 
located on the Navajo Indian Reservation within BIA Fort Defiance Agency, in Navajo 
County’s Supervisor District No. 2. The Funds shall be used to purchase and deliver the 
Roadway Signs to BIA Route N15, Dilkon, Arizona.  
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted from 
projects within this area or allocated to projects outside this area. The expenditure of the 
Allocated Funds shall only include the acquisition of road way signage. 
The BIA warrants and represents that the material purchased with the Allocated Funds 
will be used primarily for maintenance activities on roads within District 2, which are 
open to the public and are eligible for the expenditure of HURF monies under the 
Arizona Constitution and related Arizona Revised Statutes. The BIA to fully cooperate in 
any audit of the Allocated Funds by the Arizona Auditor General.  
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of June 30, 2019 shall no longer be available 
for distribution pursuant to this Memorandum of Agreement. The Allocated Funds under 
this agreement shall be paid by Navajo County directly to the invoicing party upon the 
County’s receipt of invoices or billing statement that show that the items have been 
purchased and approved by the BIA. The total of all invoices shall not exceed the 
approved amount from this agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.b.        
Meeting Date: 04/09/2019  
Title: Award of Contract B19-01-009 Woodruff Concrete Bridge Repairs
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract B19-01-009 Woodruff Concrete Bridge Repairs to Protech
Coatings, Inc. dba Infrastructure Maintenance Services.
Background:
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 PM M.S.T., March 6, 2019 in strict accordance with the
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works.  No bids
were accepted after 3:00 PM M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 PM.
Three bids were received and Protech Coatings, Inc. dba Infrastructure Maintenance Services is
the lowest responsible, responsive bidder.  The County Engineer (William R. Bess, P.E., CFM),
Project Engineer (Jerry Canon, P.E.), and Sr. Engineering Project Manager II (Bryan Cook) have
reviewed the contractor's experience in utilizing the polyester polmer concrete (PPC) overlay
systems and are all in concurrence that this contractor has sufficient experience to complete this
project.
Recommendation: 
Staff is recommending that the contract be awarded to Protech Coatings, Inc. dba Infrastructure
Maintenance Services.

B19-01-009  Woodruff Concret e Br idge Repair s  
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of April 9, 2019 (the “Effective Date”) by and between Navajo 
County (“County”) and Protech Coatings, Inc. dba Infrastructure Maintenance Services (“Contractor”). 
1. SCOPE OF WORK:  
Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and labor required for performing all work 
for WOODRUFF CONCRETE BRIDGE REPAI RS , #B19 -01 -009 , in accordance with the Contract Documents, 
in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of County through its Engineers. 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS:  
The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the attached  Notice of Invitation for 
Bids,  Specifications,  General Provisions,  Special Provisions, Proposal dated 
March 4, 2019 ,  Bidding Schedule,  Bid Bond,  Performance Bond,  No Collusion 
Affidavit,  Subcontracting Certification,  Plans, and addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any 
conflict in the documents, this Contract shall control over all others; the Specifications shall 
control over the General Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall 
control over the General Provisions. 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION:  
Contractor shall furnish all materials, equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract 
Documents free and clear of all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, 
stated in the Proposal. 
4. CANCELLATION:  
This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38 -511 (concerning conflicts of 
interest). 
5. PAYMENTS:   
Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the awarded items in the Bidding 
Schedule for Contract #B19-01-009.  Contractor shall submit invoices to County for all work 
performed hereunder.  No later than 14 days after each invoice has been verified and approved by 
the Engineer, County shall pay to Contractor ninety (90%) of the value of said work in place, as 
approved by the Engineer.  The balance of ten percent (10%) shall be retained by the Owner until the 
time of final payment and acceptance of said work, as per A.R.S. §34-221.  The provisions of A.R.S. 
§34-221 are incorporated herein by this reference and shall control in regard to all matters of 
payment. 
6. CERTIFICATIONS: 
a. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant compliance with all 
federal immigration laws and regulations relating to their employees and compliance with 
the E-verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23-214(A).  Contractor's or any subcontractor's 
breach of this warranty shall be deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in 
termination of the Contract by County.  County may randomly inspect the books and records
